108	1. **vc-x1 push: body starting with `-` breaks `commit-app`.**
109	   Push builds `jj commit -m "<title>" -m "<body>"` in the
110	   space form, so a body whose first line is a bullet
111	   (`- file: …`, the normal app-repo body shape) makes jj's
112	   parser reject the value as a stray flag. Hit on 0.62.0-5.
113	   The rollback was clean, but the push can't land a
114	   standard bullet-first body.
115	   - Fix: invoke `jj commit -m=<body>` (equals form) or set
116	     `allow_hyphen_values` on the message args.
117	   - The same defect bites a raw `jj commit -m "<body>"`, so
118	     the equals form is the general remedy.
119	   - Workaround until fixed: prepend a non-dash intro line
120	     to the body (a Prose-Form intro sentence).
121	2. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
122	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
123	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
124	   each side:
125	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
126	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
127	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
128	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
129	     all N code commits

59	## Concerns
60	
61	### 5. List-valued CLI-vs-config "wins" semantics deserves an explicit callout
62	
63	**Critique:** `audit.md:697-699` says: "If user passes
64	any `--init-from*` on CLI, the config-pinned list is
65	ignored — match the broader chain rule."
66	
67	The "broader chain rule" is about *scalar* values where
68	"wins" unambiguously means "replaces." For list-valued
69	axes, the bot thinks users coming from `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`
70	/ `PATH` will expect merge, not replace. The replace
71	choice is defensible (predictability beats expressivity)
72	but it's not the obvious choice.
73	
74	**Proposed action:** keep the replace semantics, but add
75	an explicit "list-typed-axis rule" sentence — "for
76	list-valued axes like Copying, CLI replaces config; to
77	merge, pass the config globs on CLI too." Prevents the
78	same conversation in 0.62.0.
79	
80	**Status:** open. Docs-only edit.
81	
82	### 6. Gap-list ordering hides one structural dependency
83	
84	**Critique:** `audit.md:1010-1080` ranks gaps by "blast
85	radius." But Gap #7 (`default_scope` broken-dual
86	detection) is a *prerequisite* for Gap #9 (copying
87	mechanism), per `copying.md:81-92` — copying defers
88	validation to the first downstream subcommand, which
89	means broken-dual detection has to exist first.
90	
91	**Proposed action:** one-line note above the list:
92	"ranking is rough; some structural prerequisites (e.g.
93	#7 before #9) override size ordering." Doesn't require
94	renumbering.
95	
96	**Status:** open. Docs-only edit.
97

1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	## Cycles
4	
5	A cycle has three phases:
6	
7	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
8	  first commit. Sets up the cycle:
9	  - Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.
10	  - Bump `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
11	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically via `## Priorities`)
12	    into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
13	    statement + plan ladder).
14	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
15	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
16	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
17	  needs; each runs through the
18	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
19	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
20	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
21	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
22	    `## Done`.
23	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
24	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
25	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
26	    changed.
27	
28	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
29	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
30	result must always be published at close-out. See
31	[Pushing](#pushing).
32	
33	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
34	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
35	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
36	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
37	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
38	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
39	for the local-ladder mechanics.
40	
41	## Chores sections
42	
43	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
44	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording one cycle:
45	
46	- One section per cycle is the default.
47	- Per-commit sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a
48	  deliberate alternative.
49	
50	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
51	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
52	with the cycle's anticipated close-out title (e.g.
53	`## refactor: foo bar (X.Y.Z)`). The body is empty;
54	content arrives at close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out)).
55	
56	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
57	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
58	CLAUDE.md [Chores conventions](../CLAUDE.md#chores-conventions).
59	
60	## Preparation
61	
62	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
63	
64	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores section
65	  `Commits:` ref** — see
66	  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).
67	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
68	- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one).
69	  Move into `## In Progress` as:
70	  - A **bold title line** — exact match of the chores
71	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
72	  - A **succinct problem statement**.
73	  - A **plan ladder**.
74	
75	  See [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form). A Todo that
76	  duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process
77	  bug.
78	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
79	  append a `##` header with the cycle's anticipated
80	  close-out title.
81	
82	## Work-N
83	
84	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
85	implement the change. As many as needed:
86	
87	- Each commit runs through the
88	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
89	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
90	  visibility).
91	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
92	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
93	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
94	  scope grows enough (see
95	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
96	
97	## Close-out
98	
99	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
100	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
101	what happens post-squash:
102	
103	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
104	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
105	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
106	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
107	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
108	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
109	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
110	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
111	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
112	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
113	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
114	    back-reference.
115	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
116	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
117	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
118	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
119	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
120	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
121	
122	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
123	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
124	see [Pushing](#pushing).
125	
126	## Numbering
127	
128	The version suffix on each commit encodes its phase —
129	the **final identifier `0` marks a Preparation**:
130	
131	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
132	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
133	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
134	
135	Disambiguation:
136	
137	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
138	  Preparation.
139	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
140	  identifier `0`).
141	
142	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
143	
144	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
145	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
146	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
147	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
148	
149	Sub-cycles needing no Preparation omit `.0` (`-3.1`,
150	`-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later adds
151	`-3.0` without renumbering siblings.
152	
153	Bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase so `vc-x1 -V`
154	reports the active phase at build time.
155	
156	## Per-commit flow
157	
158	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
159	through:
160	
161	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
162	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
163	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
164	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
165	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
166	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
167	4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full
168	   cycle mandatory at close-out):
169	   1. `cargo fmt`
170	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
171	   3. `cargo test`
172	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
173	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
174	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
175	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
176	   changes and we iterate until complete.
177	6. **Write the commit description** — see
178	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
179	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
180	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
181	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
182	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
183	   keeps the verb visible):
184	
185	   ```
186	   jj commit -m \
187	   "<type>: <short description> (<version>)" \
188	   -m "<intro paragraph>
189	
190	   - file1: gist
191	   - file2: gist
192	
193	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
194	   -R .
195	   ```
196	
197	**Two overrides apply:**
198	
199	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
200	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
201	  surface it; don't push through.
202	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
203	  a review or question forward.
204	
205	## Commit description
206	
207	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/)
208	with a version suffix:
209	
210	```
211	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
212	```
213	
214	### Title
215	
216	- ≤50 chars total (version suffix counts).
217	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
218	  `docs`, `chore`.
219	- Favor terse phrasings.
220	
221	### Body
222	
223	[Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
224	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
225	
226	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
227	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
228	  files with multiple distinct changes:
229	
230	  ```
231	  - path/to/file1
232	    - first distinct change
233	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
234	       with continuation indented 5
235	  ```
236	
237	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
238	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
239	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
240	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
241	
242	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
243	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
244	
245	### Trailer
246	
247	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
248	[ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).
249	
250	## Reviewing changes
251	
252	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
253	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
254	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
255	
256	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
257	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
258	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
259	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
260	
261	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
262	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
263	`jj show -r @-`.
264	
265	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
266	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
267	are in [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).
268	
269	## Pushing
270	
271	### Policy
272	
273	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
274	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
275	the cycle's result must be published.
276	
277	### Shape at close-out push
278	
279	Decide the shape and get user approval before pushing —
280	the choice is hard to undo once on the target. Surface
281	the options, wait for the user.
282	
283	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
284	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
285	  focused on one or two files.
286	- **Merge non-ff** — `main` gains a merge commit
287	  (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via two
288	  parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
289	  shape. Set up: `jj rebase -r <tip> --onto <prev>
290	  --onto <sub-tip>` where `<prev>` is the previous
291	  cycle's closed-out commit (current main tip). Two
292	  `--onto` → `<tip>` becomes a merge of `<prev>` +
293	  `<sub-tip>`.
294	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
295	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
296	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
297	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
298	
299	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
300	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
301	
302	### vc-x1 push wrapper
303	
304	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
305	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
306	
307	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
308	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
309	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
310	planned:
311	
312	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
313	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
314	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
315	
316	### .claude cadence
317	
318	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
319	with every code commit in that push.
320	
321	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
322	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
323	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
324	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
325	
326	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
327	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
328	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
329	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
330	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
331	
332	### Bot communication at the reviews
333	
334	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
335	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
336	
337	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
338	  "Work complete. Please review."
339	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
340	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
341	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
342	  --body ...` invocation by default.
343	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
344	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
345	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
346	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
347	
348	### After finalize: stop and wait
349	
350	After `vc-x1 finalize` launches (mid-session per-push or
351	at session-end), you **MUST NEVER** proceed (next step,
352	edit, tool call, text output) until the user explicitly
353	directs you to continue. **Even when the next step seems
354	obvious — wait.** Treat finalize as a hard stop for the
355	whole turn; any final words go in the approval prompt
356	*before* executing finalize. Auto-proceeding bypasses
357	the push+finalize checkpoint the user controls.
358	
359	### Recovery
360	
361	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
362	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
363	  finalize by hand:
364	
365	  ```
366	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
367	  ```
368	
369	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
370	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
371	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
372	  stale stage.
373	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
374	  (e.g. updating CLAUDE.md or memory) requires `jj
375	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
376	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
377	  push.
378	
379	## ochid trailers
380	
381	A **chid** is jj's change ID — a permanent identifier
382	that survives rebases and describes (see
383	[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md)). An **ochid** trailer
384	on a commit body links it to its counterpart in the
385	other repo via that counterpart's chid, written as a
386	workspace-root-relative path. See
387	[`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) for the
388	multi-line trailer design (multi-user / forking).
389	
390	- **Code-side commits** each carry one
391	  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
392	  change ID.
393	- **The `.claude` commit** has one `ochid: /<code-chid>`
394	  per code commit in that push. More than one occurs on
395	  Merge non-ff close-out (one ochid per Work commit in
396	  the cycle).
397	
398	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change
399	IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
400	
401	## Iterative work
402	
403	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
404	from incremental review, loop:
405	
406	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
407	2. Make the next round of changes.
408	3. User reviews the round (see
409	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
410	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
411	   new empty `@`.
412	5. If not done, go to step 2.
413	
414	Same jj mechanics as a
415	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
416	single-commit scope — the cycle's version suffix
417	doesn't change.
418	
419	## Sub-cycle ladders
420	
421	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
422	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
423	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
424	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
425	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
426	for review and bisection only.
427	
428	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
429	
430	For each Work commit in the ladder:
431	
432	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
433	2. Do the commit's work.
434	3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable** — build
435	   and clippy alone miss regressions until a later
436	   commit runs the full suite, raising bisection cost.
437	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
438	   only (no version suffix); the sub-cycle Close-out
439	   collects everything into one final commit with the
440	   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker.
441	
442	### Navigating the ladder
443	
444	Common moves:
445	
446	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
447	  from its base.
448	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
449	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
450	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
451	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
452	  isolation.
453	
454	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
455	descendants auto-rebase.
456	
457	### Close-out: squash the ladder
458	
459	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
460	
461	```
462	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
463	```
464	
465	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
466	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
467	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
468	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
469	
470	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
471	
472	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
473	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
474	
475	### Recovery
476	
477	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
478	prior commits:
479	
480	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
481	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
482	  parent.
483	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
484	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
485	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
486	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
487	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
488	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
489	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
490	
491	# References
492	
493	- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives
494	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
495	- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid
496	  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).
497	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
498	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
499	  for squash-recipe experiments.
500	- 0.41.1-6.5 cycle — first multi-commit ladder usage. The
501	  per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate originated there
502	  after a regression introduced in an early commit wasn't
503	  caught until a later one ran the full suite.
504	
```

660	- **Today** — `--private` flag.
661	- **Surface** — `--private`.
662	- **Scope** — only meaningful when Remote = `github-create`.
663	- **Gap** — none structurally; the flag is independent
664	  and orthogonal.
665	
666	**Decisions (0.61.0):**
667	
668	- **Default: `public`** (preserves today's behavior).
669	  User explicitly chose default-public over both
670	  strict-explicit-required and default-private; rationale
671	  is the user's personal context (no paid private plan)
672	  and back-compat. Consistent with the broader "defaults
673	  where natural" principle.
674	- **Visibility via `--visibility=<public|private>`** —
675	  one value-bearing flag, mirroring the Topology `--mode`
676	  decision (a single flag can't conflict with itself, so
677	  no "exactly-one-of" enforcement). Default `public`.
678	  Needed because `[default].private = true` is settable
679	  in user-config, so a per-invocation spelling for
680	  "public" must exist; `--visibility=public` provides it.
681	  `--private` / `--public` kept as optional boolean
682	  shortcuts (back-compat — `--private` exists today).
683	- **`init`-only**. Visibility sets a newly-provisioned
684	  GitHub repo's public/private, so it's meaningless on
685	  `clone` (clones existing repos, never provisions) and
686	  on every runtime subcommand.
687	- **Errors when category != github-ish** (per Remote
688	  decision; no silent ignore).
689	- **Resolution chain**: CLI > ENV > Local > Global >
690	  baked-in default (`public`). No error floor.
691	
692	#### Copying (today: `--use-template`; designed: `--init-from`)
693	
694	- **States** — `none` (default) | `code-only <path>` |
695	  `code-and-bot <path,path>`.
696	- **Today** — `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>`. Second value
697	  is dual-only.
698	- **Surface** — `--use-template`.
699	- **Gap** — none structurally; per-side templating is
700	  naturally Topology-aware. Bot-side template under
701	  Topology=single is meaningless and rejected today.
702	
703	**Decisions (0.61.0):**
704	
705	This axis is renamed **Copying** and significantly
706	expanded in scope — see [`notes/design-cli/copying.md`](copying.md) [[3]].
707	The decisions below are the axis-level summary;
708	behavior details live in the design stub.
709	
710	- **Surface**: a single `--init-from` (no per-side split
711	  — that scope is the Topology axis `--mode` owns). Takes
712	  a shell glob or an `@<file>` manifest. Recursion follows
713	  the operand (a directory recurses), so there are no
714	  `-recursive` variants. May be specified multiple times
715	  (additive). See the stub for the dual-from-one-tree
716	  rule.
717	- **Subsumes** `--use-template`, `--config <path>`, and
718	  the hypothetical `--gitignore <path>`.
719	- **Pure file copy**. No variable substitution
720	  (deliberately — that's a different feature).
721	- **Last-writer-wins + warning on collision** when
722	  multiple sources resolve to the same destination.
723	- **Canned writes suppressed when copying engaged**.
724	  Any `--init-from` present → `init` skips the canned
725	  `.vc-config.toml` + `.gitignore` writes entirely.
726	- **Deferred dual validation**. Missing `.vc-config.toml`
727	  after copy is a warning at `init`; first downstream
728	  subcommand errors. Lets users supply config post-init.
729	- **Fixed filename**. `.vc-config.toml` is the only
730	  workspace-config name; overriding is deferred.
731	- **Symlink + exec-bit preservation** (`cp -a`
732	  semantics).
733	- **Source-not-found = hard error** (typo protection).
734	  Empty glob expansion is fine.
735	- **Config-pinnable** as a list-of-strings:
736	  `[default].init-from = ["~/tpl/*", "~/tpl/.gitignore"]`.
737	  Requires a small list-of-strings extension to the
738	  config parser.
739	- **CLI overrides config entirely** (no union). If user
740	  passes any `--init-from` on CLI, the config-pinned
741	  list is ignored — match the broader chain rule.
742	- **Env-var**: colon-separated for multi-value
743	  (`VC_X1_INIT_FROM=a:b:c`), matches `PATH`
744	  convention.
745	- **Default: `none`** (no copying). Resolution chain
746	  ends in baked-in default.
747	
748	#### Scaffolding (jj init, `.gitignore`)

```
vc-x1 0.62.0-7

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)

      --no-finalize
          Stop before `finalize-claude` so it can be run manually

      --dry-run
          Print the exact commands for every stage without side effects

      --title <STR>
          Commit title (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

      --body <STR>
          Commit body (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

  -y, --yes
          Auto-approve interactive prompts (review gate, message-edit
          confirmation). Required when `--title` / `--body` aren't both supplied
          in non-interactive contexts

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

> AGENT

> DEVELOPER

As it stands our current pre-commit doesn't check non-code things, but I can see adding validate-todo and to the pre-commit in which case that would be added as an exception. Seems to be we have one rule and no exception for "doc" only changes, my initial reaction. Thoughts?

> AGENT

Your instinct is right, and I think the "one rule, no exception" framing is the better target. Here's my reasoning:

**Why the exception exists today** — the pre-commit is *only* the cargo cycle (fmt/clippy/test/install), which checks code. On a docs-only commit there's no code to check, so "skip-able" was a reasonable carve-out. But that exact carve-out is what let me skip checks on `-7`/`-8` until you asked — exceptions are where things slip.

**Why your idea closes the gap** — once the pre-commit also runs `validate-todo` (and, later, a `validate-repo` that checks anchors / chores-header sync — already foreshadowed in CLAUDE.md), there *is* something to validate on a docs commit. At that point "skip-able for docs" stops making sense: the rule becomes "always run pre-commit before review," full stop. One rule, no exception.

So I'd frame it as: **the exception was a symptom of an under-powered pre-commit, not a real property of docs commits.** Fix the pre-commit and the exception dissolves on its own — cleaner than documenting a carve-out.

**One scoping wrinkle** — "the pre-commit" lives in two places:
- the *documented* flow in `cycle-protocol.md` (step 4) + the CLAUDE.md summary — pure docs to change.
- `vc-x1 push`'s `preflight` *stage* (in `push.rs`) — adding `validate-todo` there is a **code** change, and it'd want a test.

So the full idea is one docs edit + one code change. My recommendation for decomposition:

1. **Now / cheap (docs):** adopt the single-rule framing — drop "skip-able for purely-docs," state pre-commit runs before review, and add the "push preflight is a redundant safety-net" note.
2. **Follow-up (code, its own Todo):** add `validate-todo` (then `validate-repo`) to both the documented pre-commit and push's `preflight` stage, with a test.

Doing #1 immediately means docs commits run the cargo cycle that, for now, mainly confirms the version bump builds/installs (`vc-x1 -V`) — thin but not worthless, and it stops the skip. #2 makes the rule genuinely meaningful for docs.

And is the interim state in #1 (single rule, but pre-commit not yet validating docs) acceptable to you, or would you rather hold the whole thing until #2 lands together?

> AGENT

Grounded now: `main@origin` is `0.62.0-7`, and the chain is `0.61.0 → 0.62.0-0 → … → 0.62.0-7` — all eight `0.62.0-x` commits are **pushed**. So here's my honest read of the 0.61.1-insert idea:

**The mechanics work, but the cost isn't conflicts — it's a published-history rewrite.** Inserting `0.61.1` between `0.61.0` and `0.62.0-0` means rebasing all 8 published `0.62.0-x` commits (plus their 8 `.claude` counterparts) onto the new commit, then **force-pushing both repos**. You're right that auto-rebase would sail through — CLAUDE.md is untouched in `0.62.0-x` so there's no textual conflict, and chids are stable so the `ochid:` linkage survives. But conflicts were never the risk; rewriting 16 already-published commits across two repos is, and the protocol explicitly flags remote rewrites as approval-gated, careful operations.

**And `0.61.1` is semantically arbitrary anyway** — the process-rule change isn't a patch to `0.61.0`'s por/dual content; it's independent of both cycles. So slotting it *below* buys you a rewrite *and* an arbitrary version label.

**The goal you're after — its own version slot, max-review ladder left clean — is cheaper to get on top:**

- **`0.62.1` as a fresh patch cycle after `0.62.0` close-out** — correct ordering (`0.62.1 > 0.62.0`), no rewrite, genuinely independent slot. This is the clean version of your instinct.
- **Fold as `0.62.0-9`** — simplest, no rewrite; only cost is the max-review ladder picks up one process commit (mild, and it's still docs).

For a process-doc change, *where it sits in history carries no value* — it just needs to be on `main`. So I'd skip the rewrite entirely and go `0.62.1`-on-top (if you want the clean slot) or `-9` (if you want it done now). My lean: **fold as `-9`** — it's a few lines of CLAUDE.md + cycle-protocol, and a separate cycle is ceremony for a change this small.

Want me to draft the `-9` edit, or set it up as `0.62.1` for after close-out?

> DEVELOPER

0.62.1 is cleaner and we'll go that route for now, (add as top of P1). But there is another issue
that comes to mind, our protocol of have version numbers in Titles/Bodies/todo/done/... is wrong.
Version number are subject to change. For instance on a "public" repo, like linux, the versions
numbers are defined only when a change is merged into "main" and we can't anticipate what
version a change we're working on now would land with.

So this protocol is wrong, I'm no idea what we should do but add "version number protocol"
to our Todo::Ideas list.

113	1. **vc-x1 push: body starting with `-` breaks `commit-app`.**
114	   Push builds `jj commit -m "<title>" -m "<body>"` in the
115	   space form, so a body whose first line is a bullet
116	   (`- file: …`, the normal app-repo body shape) makes jj's
117	   parser reject the value as a stray flag. Hit on 0.62.0-5.
118	   The rollback was clean, but the push can't land a
119	   standard bullet-first body.
120	   - Fix: invoke `jj commit -m=<body>` (equals form) or set
121	     `allow_hyphen_values` on the message args.
122	   - The same defect bites a raw `jj commit -m "<body>"`, so
123	     the equals form is the general remedy.
124	   - Workaround until fixed: prepend a non-dash intro line
125	     to the body (a Prose-Form intro sentence).
126	2. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
127	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
128	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
129	   each side:
130	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
131	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
132	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
133	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
134	     all N code commits
135	
136	   Teach push to:
137	   - detect this shape
138	   - skip `commit-app` when code WC is empty
139	   - compose a `.claude`-specific message

  "new_string": "1. **vc-x1 push: validate body opens with an intro paragraph.**\n   A body whose first line is a bullet (`- file: …`) is a\n   Prose-Form violation — bodies must open with an intro\n   paragraph, then bullets. Today such a body trips jj's arg\n   parser (`jj commit -m \"<body>\"` reads the leading `-` as a\n   stray flag) and push fails with an opaque error. Hit on\n   0.62.0-5.\n   - Feature, not a parser bug (reframed): push should\n     *validate* the body opens with a non-dash intro line and\n     flag its absence with a clear, specific error pointing at\n     the offending first line — rather than letting jj emit a\n     confusing one, or quietly accepting a bullet-first body.\n   - Enforcing the intro is the intended behavior, matching\n     the Prose-Form convention; we are not \"fixing\" the parser\n     to accept bullet-first bodies.\n   - Workaround until the explicit check lands: prepend a\n     non-dash intro sentence to the body."
}

945	#### Por's view of the chain
946	
947	Following the decisions in this cycle:
948	
949	- **Workspace `.vc-config.toml`** (layer 3) — dual-only.
950	  `single` never reads, never creates. Today's degenerate
951	  `[workspace] path = "/"` write is residue from when the
952	  flag was a half-implemented boolean opt-out and should
953	  be dropped.
954	- **User-config `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`**
955	  (layer 4) — topology-neutral. Both single and dual
956	  consult it.
957	- **Runtime `--mode=single`** — overrides workspace
958	  topology detection across every subcommand (not just
959	  `init`/`clone`). Treats the target repo (`.` by
960	  default, or `-R/--repo <path>`) as single: no sibling
961	  traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo` lookup, no
962	  cross-repo ochid validation. Symmetric across code
963	  and bot:
964	  - `--mode=single -R .` (in a dual workspace) → operate
965	    on code, no sibling.
966	  - `--mode=single -R .claude` → operate on `.claude`,
967	    no sibling.
968	  - `--mode=single -R /vendored/single-repo` → operate
969	    on that repo as-is.

973	The designed-state at-a-glance view — for each
974	subcommand (rows), which parameter families it accepts
975	(columns), and a short note on what the parameter does
976	in that context.
977	
978	This is the **post-equalization target**, not today's
979	state. Today many of these cells are gaps (per the audit
980	findings); the matrix captures what 0.62.0+ equalization
981	cycles drive toward.
982	
983	**Ledger — column acronyms map to axes (or to
984	cross-cutting flag groups):**
985	
986	| Acronym | Axis / group | Flags |
987	| --- | --- | --- |
988	| **T** | Topology | `--mode=<single\|dual>` (creation on `init`/`clone`; runtime override on every other subcommand) |
989	| **A/R** | Remote | `--account` + `--repo` (remote provider resolution; user-config-keyed) |
990	| **Priv** | Privacy | `--visibility=<public\|private>` (GitHub visibility; `init`-only) |
991	| **CP** | Copying | `--init-from` (file copying into the workspace) |
992	| **CFG** | *Config paths* (not an axis) | `--config <path>` / `--global-config <path>` (point at non-default config files) |
993	| **NO-CFG** | *Config skip* (not an axis) | `--no-local-config` / `--no-global-config` (skip layers in the resolution chain) |
994	| **SC** | *Scope* (runtime selection, not an axis) | `--scope` / `-R` (per-invocation repo selection on dual workspaces) |
995	
996	The first four acronyms match feature axes (negotiated
997	in this cycle); the last three are cross-cutting flag
998	groups that don't correspond to single axes but appear on
999	many subcommands.

1005	- `—` — not applicable for this subcommand.

1026	**Footnotes:**
1027	
1028	- `clone CP*` — copying on `clone` overlays *on top of*
1029	  the cloned content (same flag set, same collision
1030	  rules as `init`).
1031	- `clone A/R —, Priv —` — clone resolves its source from
1032	  `<TARGET>` and clones existing repos; it never
1033	  provisions a remote, so the Remote-create and Privacy
1034	  axes don't apply (verified: `clone.rs` has no
1035	  provisioning path).
1036	- `push SC*` — push uses bookmark scoping rather than
1037	  `--scope`; the cell marks "yes, push reads scope-ish
1038	  info" without implying surface identity.
1039	- `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` `SC*` — these are the
1040	  desc-family outliers that bypass `for_each_repo`
1041	  today (per `## Commonality`); post-equalization
1042	  they route through scope like the rest of the
1043	  `CommonArgs` family.
1044	- `config dump` is a new subcommand to be added — emits
1045	  the baked-in default config so users can save and
1046	  modify it.
1047	
1048	**Topology column (`T`) — quick reference:**
1049	
1050	- *Creation* (init/clone): `--mode=<single|dual>` chooses
1051	  the workspace shape that gets written into
1052	  `.vc-config.toml` (dual only — single writes nothing).
1053	- *Runtime override* (other subcommands): `--mode=single`
1054	  treats the target repo (`.` by default, or
1055	  `-R/--repo <path>`) as single — no sibling
1056	  traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo` lookup.
1057	  Symmetric across code and bot. Default-dual at the
1058	  workspace level still applies (`.vc-config.toml`
1059	  still says dual); `--mode=single` is a per-invocation
1060	  override asking "treat the target as single for
1061	  this command."
1062	- `finalize` accepts T as a runtime override and SC for
1063	  per-side selection. Today's only use site is inside
1064	  `push`'s workflow against a specific repo path, but
1065	  the body itself is generic (squash + commit with
1066	  title/body + push) and works on either side
1067	  standalone — `--scope=code` or `--scope=bot`. Use
1068	  case: a user manually squashing a cycle into one
1069	  commit and pushing. `--squash` is logically optional
1070	  (auto-detect from `@` being empty); `--detach` and
1071	  `--delay` are unnecessary outside `push`'s schedule-
1072	  detached flow but still work.
1073

1076	The original pre-design gaps are now resolved by the

> AGENT

You're right — `validate-todo` is single-file (it just happened to be pointed at `todo.md`), so calling it "passes" overstates the coverage. For a pre-commit gate that's a real gap: a renumber slip in `bugs.md`, `todo-backlog.md`, or even the `## Ideas` section of `todo.md` (which now has numbered entries — #1, #2) would sail through.

Your design instinct is right, and a few observations sharpen it:

- **Rename**: `validate-todo` → `validate-numbering`, and the `fix-todo` partner → `fix-numbering`. The pair is about numbered-sequence integrity, not todos specifically. Cheap rename (single-user internal tool).
- **It's already *almost* generic.** The "1 Bugs / 45 Todo / 7 Todo" counts show it already detects numbered entries per section by the `^\d+\.␠` anchor and reports by section — the column-0 anchor is what keeps indented sub-lists (3-space) from being miscounted as entries. So the engine is most of the way there; what's todo-specific is mostly the *name* and the single-file scope.
- **"Check everything" = make the multi-file scan the default.** No-arg `validate-numbering` should walk the known notes set (`todo.md`, `todo-backlog.md`, `bugs.md` — and `done.md`/`chores-NN.md` if/when they carry numbered sections), validating *every* `#…#` section that contains `^\d+\.␠` entries, with explicit paths as an override. That's exactly what the pre-commit wants: one invocation, total coverage.
- **Bonus it'd catch for free**: `## Ideas` numbering (now live) and any *new* numbered section someone adds later — no per-section allow-list to maintain.

One thing to decide: scope as "a fixed default file list" vs. "walk `notes/**.md`." I lean fixed-list (predictable, no surprise files), but walking is more future-proof.

Where I'd put this: it's really the substance behind Todo #2's "doc validators" bullet, but it's chunky enough to be its own item. So my proposal:

- **Add a new Todo** — "validate-numbering: rename + all-files + generic section/sequence check" — and have Todo #2 reference it (wiring validators into pre-commit *depends* on this landing).

Two quick calls before I write it:
1. **Fold this capture into `0.62.0-9`** (consistent with how we just folded the Todo #1 reframe and the earlier captures), or keep `-9` as-is and capture separately?
2. **Default scope**: fixed notes-file list, or walk `notes/**.md`?

> DEVELOPER

Fold into -9; use a fixed notes-file list and rename fix-todo to fix-numbering as you suggested.
I lean towards defaulting to "notes/**.md" and follow the --init-from convention of single level "@<file-name>" with of list of files/dirs, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good — and let me reconcile the two halves of your message, because "use a fixed notes-file list" and "default to `notes/**.md`" pull opposite ways. After thinking about it I'd land on **fixed-list default**, and here's the concrete reason:

**A `notes/**.md` walk would false-positive on prose.** The generic "validate any `#…#` section with `^\d+.␠` entries" rule can't structurally tell a *managed entry sequence* from an *ordinary numbered list*. And our prose docs are full of the latter — `cycle-protocol.md`'s per-commit flow (`1. … 8.`), design notes, etc. Worse, markdown lets writers leave `1. 1. 1.` (it still renders 1-2-3), which is common and legitimate — a walk-everything validator would **block commits** on those. False-positives in a pre-commit gate are worse than a quiet miss, because they erode trust in the gate.

The files where "column-0 number == managed sequence" is actually *true* are exactly the three you named: `todo.md`, `todo-backlog.md`, `bugs.md`. That's a short, stable list — the maintenance cost of "fixed" is near-zero, and it sidesteps the prose problem entirely.

**Your `--init-from` convention is the right call for the *override*, though** — positional files/dirs (a dir → its `*.md`) plus an `@<file>` manifest, additive. That gives you the "point it at anything" flexibility for ad-hoc runs without making the *default* walk everything. Best of both: safe default, flexible override.

So: **fixed-list default + `--init-from`-style override args**, rename the pair (`validate-numbering` / `fix-numbering`), generic per-section detection. Let me capture that as a new Todo and fold it into `-9`. Reading the current Todo section to place it:

Do nothing — and not just as "it's small," but because it's the *correct* behavior, not a compromise.

**The tail is inherent and self-resolving.** The `.claude` journal accumulates continuously (CLAUDE.md even says so: "the `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active session"). Those 3 lines aren't orphaned or lost — they sit in the `.claude` working copy and get swept into the **next** `.claude` commit, whenever the next real push happens (`-10`, or the `-7`/`-8` repair). The session data always trails the code commit it talks about by exactly this kind of tail; that's the designed cadence, not a leak.

And it's turtles all the way down: any `finalize`/`push` you run to capture these 3 lines *itself* produces more session text (this exchange, my reply), so you can never drive the `.claude` WC to empty-and-committed while the session is live. Chasing it never converges.

Correct that it's manual, but let me refine the *why* — the `--ignore-immutable` gap is real but it's the secondary reason, not the main one.

**Primary reason — wrong verb/scope.** `vc-x1 finalize` (and `push`) operate on the **working copy** (`@`): "squash/commit the current state, then push." They have no mode that says "go rewrite the body of *that specific past commit* (`-7`, `-8`)." This repair is a surgical history edit of two *named, already-committed* commits — not a finalize of pending work. So finalize is simply the wrong tool here regardless of flags; there's no `finalize -r <-7>` semantics.

**Secondary reason — your point.** Even if finalize *could* target a past commit, `-7`/`-8` are immutable (ancestors of the pushed `main`), so any rewrite needs `--ignore-immutable`, which finalize doesn't expose. But that's downstream of the scope issue.

So the right tool is plain `jj describe -r <chid> -m "<corrected body>" --ignore-immutable` per commit — direct, surgical, no workflow-wrapper in the way. `jj git push --bookmark main` then force-updates each remote (finalize's `--push` is just a thin wrapper over that same `jj git push`, so it'd add nothing).

The dry preview came out right, by the way: each proposed tail ends with a single correctly-directed trailer (app→`/.claude/<bot>`, bot→`/<app>`), and `-9` is already clean (app→`/.claude/ynuxlympoqmt`, bot→`/svskxlppxznp`).

> DEVELOPER

Hmm, you say `jj git push --bookmark main` do we need to move main each time from it's current position to where our rework is happening, that feels odd but maybe a --bookmark (branch) is always needed on a push, clarify.

> AGENT

Good question — you're conflating two different things, and neither requires moving `main` to the rework. Let me separate them.

**1. We never move `main` to `-7`/`-8`.** The rework happens *below* `main`. `main` is on `-9`, and when I rewrite `-7`/`-8`, jj auto-rebases their descendants (`-8`, `-9`, the empty `@`) — and a bookmark **tracks its commit by chid across rewrites**. So `main`, sitting on `-9` (chid `svskxlppxznp`), automatically follows to the rewritten `-9'` (same chid, new git hash). I don't touch `main` at all; it stays "on `-9`," just on the rebased version of it.

```
before:  -6 ─ -7  ─ -8  ─ -9(main)
rewrite: -6 ─ -7' ─ -8' ─ -9'(main)     ← main rode along, same chid
```

**2. `--bookmark main` on push is a *selector*, not a move.** It tells `jj git push` *which* bookmark to publish to the remote — "push `main` and its ancestors to the remote `main` ref." It does not move the bookmark; it names what to send. You specify it because bare `jj git push` only pushes tracked bookmarks that changed, and being explicit (`--bookmark main`) is the safe, conventional form — it's exactly what `vc-x1 push` does internally. So it's not "always required," but it's the normal explicit way.

The one wrinkle that *is* real: because `-7'`/`-8'`/`-9'` are rewrites (new hashes), pushing `main` is a **non-fast-forward / force update** of the remote ref. jj handles that itself via bookmark tracking — it knows remote `main` = old `-9` and local `main` = new `-9'`, and updates it. No git-style `--force` flag needed; jj just reports "Move sideways bookmark main."

So: no manual `main` moves anywhere — one `jj git push --bookmark main` per repo, each a jj-managed force-update. Want me to go ahead (apply the four `jj describe` rewrites, re-verify, then push both)?

│  docs: nits N1–N4 + todo captures (0.62.0-9)
◆  ovvqwsxr wink@saville.com 2026-05-29 16:05:05 1d7384c6

> AGENT

Your mental model is actually **correct** — and that's exactly why jj renamed "branch" → "bookmark." A bookmark *is* a pointer to a single node (one commit), and the rename was deliberate: unlike a git branch, it doesn't auto-advance when you commit on top. So `--bookmark main` does name the **node** `svskxlpp` (`-9`), not a series. Good instinct.

The thing your model is missing is **git's transfer rule**, which is separate from what a bookmark *is*: pushing a ref sends the node it points at **plus every ancestor object the remote doesn't already have**. You can't put a commit on a remote without its parents — a commit is only valid if its ancestry is present. So:

- `jj git push --bookmark main` = "repoint remote `main` to my local `main`'s node (`-9'`), shipping whatever ancestor objects the remote lacks."
- The remote already has `-6` and below. After our rewrite it's *missing* `-7'`, `-8'`, `-9'` (new hashes). So those three ride along, and remote `main` moves to `-9'`. The old `-7`/`-8`/`-9` objects become unreferenced on the remote (GC'd later).

So the "series" gets transferred not because `--bookmark` means series, but because those three rewritten nodes are new objects required to make the new `main` pointer valid.

And this is the same reason `-9` is "touched" even though we only edit `-7`/`-8`: rewriting `-7` gives it a new hash → `-8'` has a new parent → new hash → `-9'` new hash. The **content and chid of `-9` are unchanged**, but its git hash changes because an ancestor did. Your `@---::main` view is precisely the set that gets new hashes:

```
vokukpxy(-7) ─ ovvqwsxr(-8) ─ svskxlpp(-9, main)
   edit body      edit body       rebased only
   → new hash     → new hash      → new hash, same chid/content
```

In short: one `jj git push --bookmark main` per repo repoints the node, and git carries the three new ancestor objects with it. Shall I proceed?

> DEVELOPER

One last clarification, how far back do a `jj git push --bookmark main` for new nodes relative to the remote, it would seem more efficient if we proivded the set of nodes we changed i.e. "@---::main" of course if we made a mistake it'd be better to know now. Educate me, please!

> AGENT

Great question — this is the heart of git's transfer model, and your efficiency instinct is right; it's just already handled for you.

**How far back: to the nearest common ancestor with the remote — not the root.** On push, the client and server do a quick **negotiation**: the server advertises the commits it already has, the client walks ancestry down from the pushed commit (`-9'`) and stops at the first ancestor the server already has. Here:

```
-9' → -8' → -7' → -6 (STOP — remote already has -6, unchanged hash)
```

So it sends exactly `-7'`, `-8'`, `-9'` — the rewritten frontier. `-6` and everything below are untouched (same hashes), so they're never reconsidered. That frontier *is* effectively your `@---::main` (minus the empty `@`), computed automatically.

**Why you don't hand it the set yourself:**
- **Push is ref-oriented, not range-oriented.** The operation is "make remote `main` = my local `-9'`"; the objects follow by *reachability*. There's no "push these N commits" API — you move a ref, git sends what that requires. So `@---::main` isn't something you'd pass; it's just a *description* of what reachability already selects.
- **Reachability is already minimal and correct**, even if 50 commits had changed — no efficiency left on the table.

**And here it's even cheaper than "3 commits" suggests.** These are *description-only* rewrites — the **trees and blobs are byte-identical** to the old `-7`/`-8`/`-9` (file content unchanged), and the remote already has those objects. So the only *new* objects are the **3 commit objects themselves** (they differ because message + parent-hash changed). Trees/blobs are reused. The actual transfer is three tiny commit headers. Maximally efficient already.

**On "better to know now if we made a mistake":** jj front-runs that — `jj git push` prints the bookmark move (`Move sideways bookmark main from <old> to <new>`) so you see the repoint before trusting it, and the local op-log (`jj op restore`) can undo the rewrite if the *local* result looks wrong. The remote force-update is the one-way door (old commits get orphaned, recoverable only until GC), which is why we previewed the four bodies first. If the remote `main` had drifted (someone else pushed), jj would detect the tracking mismatch and refuse rather than clobber.

Ready to apply the four rewrites, re-verify locally, and push — or do you want to eyeball anything else first?

> DEVELOPER

Sorry, another Q: if this was linux that "list" you mention sending could be huge 1,000's commits from some point to the root, I assume git negotiaties it smarter than here is the list I have, or I probably miss-understood.

> AGENT

You understood correctly — and your suspicion is right: git **never** ships "tip-to-root, here's everything I have." It computes the **divergence**, bounded by how far your branch has drifted from the remote, not by total history depth. Two different mechanisms depending on direction:

**Push (our case) — reachability cut, no per-commit chatter.** The receiving side advertises only its **ref tips** (bounded by number of branches/tags — thousands at most, not millions of commits). The sending client then computes, *locally*, the set:

> objects reachable from `<commit I'm pushing>` **but not** reachable from any remote tip

i.e. `git pack-objects` with the remote tips as *negative* refs (`-9' --not <remote main, all other remote tips>`). It paints everything reachable from the remote tips "uninteresting" and sends only what's left. The crucial scale trick: once a commit is painted uninteresting, **all its ancestors are too**, so the walk **stops at the shared frontier** — it never descends into the millions of common ancestors. With the **commit-graph + generation numbers**, git prunes that walk hard. So the work is O(your divergence), not O(history). In Linux, pushing 3 new commits onto an up-to-date remote computes the same tiny delta we have here.

**Fetch (the symmetric case)** is where the real *negotiation* happens, because neither side knows the other's full state. The client sends `want <hash>` then `have <hash>` lines walking back from its refs — but in **exponentially-stepping batches** (skip 1, 2, 4, 8… commits), so the server ACKs a common base in a handful of round-trips — logarithmic-ish, not linear. Again bounded by divergence.

So the "list" is always the **delta to the nearest common frontier**, found by reachability (push) or a few negotiation rounds (fetch) — never an enumeration to the root.

Our situation is just the degenerate small instance: remote and local share everything through `-6` (untouched hashes), divergence is `-7'`/`-8'`/`-9'`, so the cut yields three commit objects (and even their trees/blobs are reused). Same algorithm Linux uses; tiny inputs.

Ready to apply the four rewrites and push whenever you give the word.

842	### Resolution chain
843	
844	The per-axis sections above each say "CLI → user-config →
845	default (or error)." That's a sketch; the full resolution
846	chain has **five layers**, with the bottom varying per
847	axis. The same chain applies to **every axis** and every
848	field a config carries (account, repo category/value,
849	privacy, topology, copying sources, …) — nothing axis-
850	specific.
851	
852	#### Layers (highest precedence first)
853	
854	1. **CLI flag** — `--mode`, `--account`, `--private`,
855	   `--config <path>`, `--global-config <path>`, etc. The
856	   per-invocation surface. CLI is god — wins over every
857	   other layer when present. CLI carries *values* only;
858	   there are **no per-axis "default-X" flags** (to change
859	   a default, edit a config — local, global, or the
860	   baked-in starting point).
861	2. **Environment variable** — `VC_X1_<KEY>`. The
862	   per-session surface, set via `export` or one-shot
863	   prefix. Loses to CLI.
864	3. **Local config** — `./.vc-config.toml` (the workspace
865	   metadata file), or whatever `--config <path>` resolved
866	   to. Per-project durable intent. Loses to env-var and
867	   CLI; **may carry any field a CLI flag exposes** — no
868	   carve-outs (a local config can pin
869	   `[global] config-path` just as it pins `account`).

## docs: apply max review #1 (0.62.0)

Commits: TBD (backfills at next cycle's Preparation)

Apply accepted items from
[`max-review-1.md`](../design-cli/max-review-1.md) to
the por/dual parity audit doc. The review file is the
cycle's working list: `0.62.0-1` seeds the full
capture, each later `-N` applies one item and deletes
it from the list (git history holds the original).

- **Concern #1 — runtime `--por` semantics** (0.62.0-2).
  Three doc-text spots (Topology Decisions "Runtime
  override" bullet, "Por's view of the chain" bullet,
  matrix Topology-column sub-bullet) read runtime
  `--por` as code-privileged (`→ Scope([Code])`, "just
  my code"). Rewrote all three to the target-relative
  rule: `--por` declares the target repo (`.` or
  `-R/--repo <path>`) single and suppresses sibling
  discovery symmetrically across code and bot — it
  doesn't privilege a side.
- **`--mode=<single|dual>` topology surface** (0.62.0-3).
  Concern #2 flagged the contradictory "exactly-one-of
  `--por` / `--dual` with default-dual" phrasing. Rather
  than reword, replaced the two boolean flags with one
  value-bearing flag `--mode=<single|dual>` (default
  `dual`, `single` ≡ por, optional `s` / `d` aliases) —
  a single flag can't conflict with itself, so the
  contradiction dissolves. Propagated across the design
  sections (Topology Decisions, Por's view, matrix +
  quick-reference, Mapping section, Gap #8); the runtime
  override from Concern #1 becomes `--mode=single`. Audit
  findings (`## 1`–`## 8`) keep `--por` — they describe
  today's code.
- **`--visibility` + clone-row fix** (0.62.0-4). Resolves
  Concern #2's Privacy half the same way `--mode` did for
  Topology: Privacy axis adopts
  `--visibility=<public|private>` (default `public`,
  `--private` / `--public` kept as shortcuts),
  **`init`-only**. Empirically confirmed (`init -h`,
  `clone -h`, `clone.rs`) that `clone` never provisions a
  remote — so the matrix's clone row is corrected from
  `A/R ✓ Priv ✓` to `—` for both, with a footnote. Closes
  Concern #2.
- **Copying surface: collapse to a single `--init-from`**
  (0.62.0-5). Concern #3 flagged the six `--init-from*`
  flags as surface doubling. The fix went further — to one
  flag for every topology.
  - Dropped the `-recursive` variants: a directory operand
    always recurses. The review's `cp`-parity rationale
    was wrong (`cp` errors on a directory without `-r`);
    the honest reason is that a non-recursive directory
    copy is meaningless here (an empty directory), so
    nothing needs an opt-in to guard.
  - Dropped the per-side `-code` / `-bot` split too — that
    scope is the Topology axis `--mode=<single|dual>`

Grounded now. The current `0.62.0` chores section confirms your read: only **Concern #1** uses the clean `**Concern #1 — …**` prefix; #2/#3/#4 are titled by their *fix* (`**--mode=…**`, `**--visibility + clone-row fix**`, `**Copying surface…**`), and #5/#6/nits/process-obs aren't written yet (that's `-7`…`-10`, this session). References to `max-review-1.md` are exactly three: `chores-12.md:370` (a link) and `todo.md:23,32` (both inside the In Progress block, which resets at close-out).

My thoughts:

**1. Reformat all items to `**Concern #N — …**` / `**Nit N1 — …**` + list everything — yes, and it's literally the close-out's job.** Close-out is defined as "move the In Progress block into the chores section + write the narrative." So completing #5/#6/nits/process-obs *and* normalizing #2–#4 to the bold-item prefix belongs in the `0.62.0` close-out commit. Scannable, and each bullet ties to its review item + commit. I'd keep the fix in the title after the prefix, e.g. `**Concern #2 (Topology) — --mode=<single|dual>** (0.62.0-3)`.

**2. Delete `max-review-1.md` — agree, and close-out is the right moment.** It's a fully-drained working list now; once the chores section holds the per-item narrative and git log holds the commits, keeping a drained tracker just invites a future "why is this empty?" It matches this project's ethos (git is the record; retire drained trackers — same as Done-retiring and ref re-packing). Doing it *at* close-out makes one coherent "close the review" act: full narrative lands, redundant list goes. Caveats:
   - **Nothing's truly lost** — `max-review-1.md` (Strengths, each item's original critique/proposed-action, Scope reviewed) lives in git history across `-1`…`-10`; `git show <sha>:…/max-review-1.md` recovers it.
   - **Reword the 3 refs**: the `chores-12.md:370` link → describe it as the now-retired working list (point at git history, not a live path); the two `todo.md` refs vanish when In Progress resets.
   - **Optional**: preserve a one-line nod to the review's *Strengths* in the chores narrative (the three-pass methodology / axis decomposition praise), since that's the one thing chores doesn't otherwise capture. Everything else is resolution-oriented and already covered.

**3. Timing/decomposition** — two shapes:
   - **A (my lean): all of it in the `0.62.0` close-out** — flesh-out + reformat + delete + standard bookkeeping (Done entry, In Progress reset, README). Meatier close-out, but coherent and protocol-aligned (the narrative *is* close-out work).
   - **B: a final `0.62.0-11`** ("docs: complete chores narrative + retire max-review-1.md") then a lean bookkeeping close-out — isolates the narrative+deletion if you'd rather the close-out stay minimal.

I'd go **A**, at close-out, with the Strengths one-liner. We push `-10` first, then I draft the close-out that way. Sound right, or do you want B / keep `max-review-1.md` / something else?

```
1	# por/dual parity + bidirectional conversion
2	
3	Forward-looking design capture (pre-design). Concrete work
4	lands when a cycle picks it up — likely on top of the
5	`--scope` rollout (the `0.50.0` work in `notes/todo.md`).
6	
7	`vc-x1` today treats the **dual** workspace (a code repo
8	plus a `.claude` bot-session repo, cross-linked by `ochid:`
9	trailers, configured by `.vc-config.toml`) as the primary
10	shape; **por** (Plain Old Repo — a single repo, no
11	`.claude/`, no `.vc-config.toml`) is supported but bolted
12	on. Two goals:
13	
14	## 1. Parity
15	
16	por and dual should be first-class equals across every
17	subcommand — same code paths, same quality of support, no
18	"dual plus an afterthought" asymmetry. The `--por` topology
19	flag (dual vs single — see `notes/todo.md`) is the
20	substrate; parity is the goal it serves.
21	
22	## 2. Bidirectional conversion
23	
24	A new capability: convert a workspace between the two
25	shapes in place.
26	
27	- **`por → dual`** — attach a `.claude` companion repo and
28	  a `.vc-config.toml` to an existing single repo; emit
29	  cross-links going forward.
30	- **`dual → por`** — detach the `.claude` companion; drop
31	  `.vc-config.toml`; the code repo continues standalone.
32	
33	## Open questions (resolve at design time)
34	
35	- Surface: a new subcommand (`vc-x1 convert`?
36	  `promote` / `demote`?) vs. flags on existing commands.
37	- `dual → por` — what happens to the `.claude` history:
38	  archived, left in place untouched, deleted? Leading
39	  guess is "left alone, just unlinked," but TBD.
40	- `.vc-config.toml` — creation on `por → dual` (which
41	  fields, defaults from user config?) and removal on
42	  `dual → por`.
43	- Whether `init` / `clone` change, or conversion is purely
44	  a separate operation on an already-existing workspace.
45	- Idempotency / re-running; recovery from a conversion
46	  interrupted mid-way.
47	
48	## See also
49	
50	- `notes/todo.md` — the por/dual item, and the `--scope`
51	  cluster it builds on.
52	- `src/options_flags/por.rs` — `PorFlag`, the `--por`
53	  topology flag.
54	- `notes/chores/chores-07.md > ## init + clone redesign (0.41.1)`
55	  — the `init_one` / `clone_one` primitives that aim to
56	  give a single chokepoint for scope-shaped changes.
57	
```

> AGENT

Read both. The key finding: they're **not** wholesale duplicates — the 56-line stub has two halves with different fates.

**The stub (`por-dual-parity.md`) is two things:**
1. **Parity** (goal #1) — "por/dual should be first-class equals." This is now **fully subsumed** by the audit doc, which is the 1200-line thorough treatment of exactly this. Redundant.
2. **Bidirectional conversion** (goal #2) — `por → dual` (attach `.claude` + `.vc-config.toml`) and `dual → por` (detach), plus open design questions (surface verb, `.claude` history handling, idempotency). This is **not in the audit doc at all** — the audit is about *equalizing existing* commands, not the *new* "transform a workspace in place" capability. And it's a **live item**: `todo.md` Todo #7 (`por → dual conversion`, your P3) points at this file as its design home.

So your instinct is right that the *current* two files overlap — but the overlap is only the parity half. The fix isn't "delete to one file," it's:

**Recommendation: refocus the stub to conversion-only, and rename it `por-dual-conversion.md`.** Drop the redundant Parity section (audit owns it), keep the conversion design + open questions, repoint the refs. Result: **two single-concern files** — `por-dual-parity-audit.md` (parity, the deep one) and `por-dual-conversion.md` (the distinct future feature) — instead of two that overlap. That's the "correct 2" form: not two files on the same topic, but one file per concern.

**Why not just merge conversion into the audit (true 1-file):** the audit's own Status pins it as a *"pre-implementation design snapshot"* of the 0.61.0 parity work. Conversion is a forward-looking, not-yet-designed *different* feature. Folding it in bloats a 1200-line doc and muddies its identity/tense. The clean separation is worth one small file.

If you still want literally one file, the fallback is: add a `## Bidirectional conversion (future)` section to the audit and delete the stub — but I'd advise against it for the reason above.

**Timing:** not in the `0.62.0` close-out — that's already meaty (chores flesh-out + `max-review-1.md` deletion); keep it focused on closing the review. The parity/conversion refactor is its own small commit — either just before close-out, or a `0.62.1`/`0.63.0` tidy. On rename I'd update the live refs (`todo.md` `[1]` def + Todo #7 / P3); the `chores-10/11/12` mentions are historical record and stay as-is.

> AGENT

Three distinct threads here, escalating in ambition. Let me take them honestly, because they don't all point the same way.

**1. The doc name — agreed, "audit" undersells it, and the real issue is it's two documents fused.** Its own Status says it captures "the audit findings *and* the negotiated design." Those have different lifecycles:
- The **audit** (`## 1`–`## 8`) is *frozen evidence* — true as of commit `463e6bd9`, retrospective, never changes.
- The **design** (feature axes, decisions, resolution chain, matrix, gap list) is a *living target* — 0.62.0+ cycles mutate it every time.

Fusing a frozen snapshot with a living plan in one 1200-line file is the actual smell. The clean refactor isn't just a rename — it's a **split**: `…-audit.md` (the snapshot, stays put) + `…-design.md` (the living target). And the design half *is* the "design document for the redesign" you're reaching for — it's just currently buried under a name that says "audit."

**2. vc-x2 — I'd push back on a rewrite, and here your own audit is the evidence.** The Commonality pass found the architecture is *sound*: the codebase already has the right scope pattern, and "por is bolted on everywhere the pattern wasn't applied." That's a strong signal to **equalize incrementally** (the gap-list cycles), not reboot — a rewrite throws away working `push`/`finalize`/`ochid`/dual-repo machinery to re-derive the same bones.

So separate two meanings of "vc-x2":
- **"Rewrite the engine"** — no; the evidence says the engine is salvageable.
- **"Reposition the product under a bigger mission"** — maybe, but that's driven by thread 3, not by the parity work. The deciding question is: *does the viewer vision change the core architecture* (data model, an index/daemon, persistence) enough to warrant a clean slate? Answer that first; the name follows the architecture, not the reverse.

**3. The side-by-side conversation+code viewer — this is the actually-big idea, and it reframes everything.** Notice: the dual-repo + `ochid:` design *already is* the substrate for it. The cross-links make "given a code commit → its conversation" (and back) a solved data problem. The viewer is just the UI that surfaces a link you've already been carefully maintaining. That's the payoff of all the ochid discipline — provenance: seeing *why* code changed, not just what.

My leanings on building it:
- **Keep the hard part in `vc`, editor-agnostic.** "Resolve an ochid, assemble the paired code-diff + conversation slice for a commit" is engine work — it extends the existing `show`/`chid`/`desc` family. Make that a library/command. Then any front-end (Zed, VSCode, a local web view, even a TUI) is a *thin* presentation layer over it. This hedges you against any single editor's API.
- **Zed vs VSCode — a real constraint to verify, not assume.** Zed aligns (Rust, your stack, your preference), but last I knew its extension API is young and doesn't expose arbitrary custom webview/panel UIs the way VSCode's does — a rich side-by-side panel might exceed what Zed extensions can currently do, while VSCode webviews handle it easily. Worth checking Zed's *current* extension surface before betting on it (my knowledge here has a cutoff). An editor-agnostic core means you don't have to bet — start with a standalone view, wrap it per-editor later.

**Where this leaves us:** the parity work is one chapter of a larger story — "vc as a code+conversation provenance tool." That arguably wants a **top-level vision/design doc** sitting *above* the parity-audit and conversion docs, framing the direction (dual-repo + ochid → provenance → viewer). That's probably the "design document" you actually want to create.

These are all forward-looking — I'd **capture them as `## Ideas`** (or seed a `notes/design-cli/vision.md`) rather than act now, so we don't lose them but also don't derail the `0.62.0` close-out that's still pending. Want me to draft those captures, and which form — Ideas entries, or a starter vision doc?

> DEVELOPER

Good feed back, capture this in Ideas and what to do about a rename/split, por-dual-parity-audit.md doesn't work for me, we need to rename/split in this close-out or 0.63.0. Your preference.

> DEVELOPER

Good feed back, capture this "grander ambition" in Ideas, but what to do about a rename/split, por-dual-parity-audit.md doesn't work for me, we need to rename/split in this close-out or 0.63.0. Your preference.

> AGENT

**My preference: do the rename/split in `0.63.0`, not the close-out.** Reasons:

- **Close-out is defined as bookkeeping-only.** Splitting a 1200-line doc — deciding what's frozen-audit vs living-design, moving the content, re-anchoring — is substantive work, not bookkeeping. It doesn't belong in a close-out commit by the protocol's own rule.
- **It's anchor-risky and deserves a validation pass.** The audit is cross-referenced internally (the Reading guide I just added, many `[N]` refs) and externally (`copying.md`, `todo.md`, `chores-12`). A split will break links if done carelessly; that wants a focused cycle, not a close-out rider.
- **It pairs naturally with the parity→conversion refocus.** Both are "restructure the design-cli docs" — `0.63.0` can be exactly that cycle (audit split/rename + stub → `por-dual-conversion.md`), while the `0.62.0` close-out stays focused on *closing the review* (chores flesh-out + `max-review-1.md` deletion, both review-tied).

And the name dislike likely *resolves itself* in the split: once the frozen evidence and the living design are separate files, the audit-named one is genuinely just an audit (accurate), and the design half gets a design-appropriate name (could even reclaim `por-dual-parity.md` once the stub becomes `por-dual-conversion.md`). We settle exact names in `0.63.0`.

**For the captures**, I'll add two `## Ideas` entries (these fold into the close-out — Ideas-capture *is* close-out's job):

1. **vc as a code+conversation provenance tool** (the grander ambition) — dual-repo + `ochid` is already the substrate; the viewer surfaces an already-solved link. Keep resolution/assembly in `vc` (editor-agnostic engine, extends `show`/`chid`/`desc`); editor add-on is a thin layer; Zed-leaning but verify its extension API supports a rich side-by-side panel. `vc-x2` only if the viewer changes the *core* architecture — engine-rewrite is unwarranted (Commonality says equalize incrementally); product-reposition is the open question. Possible `notes/design-cli/vision.md` above the parity/conversion sub-designs.
2. **Restructure the design-cli parity docs (target 0.63.0)** — split `…-audit.md` into frozen-audit + living-design; refocus the stub to conversion-only + rename; repoint refs and validate anchors; deferred from close-out for the reasons above.

Want me to **start the `0.62.0` close-out now** with those two Ideas folded in (alongside the chores flesh-out and `max-review-1.md` deletion)?
